Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

read · completed

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.\n\n(End of file - total 36 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

417: +// eventLog collects the interface events of a registry by interface name.
418: +type eventLog struct {
419: +	mu      sync.Mutex
420: +	actions map[string][]string
421: +}
422: +
423: +func collectEvents(ctx context.Context, events <-chan aggregatedEvent) *eventLog {
424: +	l := &eventLog{actions: map[string][]string{}}
425: +
426: +	go func() {
427: +		for {
428: +			select {
429: +			case ev := <-events:
430: +				l.mu.Lock()
431: +				name := ev.Attributes["ifname"]
432: +				l.actions[name] = append(l.actions[name], ev.Action)
433: +				l.mu.Unlock()
434: +			case <-ctx.Done():
435: +				return
436: +			}
437: +		}
438: +	}()
439: +
440: +	return l
441: +}
442: +
443: +func (l *eventLog) get(name string) []string {
444: +	l.mu.Lock()
445: +	defer l.mu.Unlock()
446: +
447: +	return slices.Clone(l.actions[name])
448: +}
449: +
450: +// waitFor waits until every name has an event with one of the actions.
451: +func (l *eventLog) waitFor(t *testing.T, names []string, actions ...string) {
452: +	t.Helper()
453: +
454: +	deadline := time.Now().Add(3 * time.Second)
455: +	for {
456: +		var missing []string
457: +		for _, name := range names {
458: +			if !slices.ContainsFunc(l.get(name), func(a string) bool {
459: +				return slices.Contains(actions, a)
460: +			}) {
461: +				missing = append(missing, name)
462: +			}
463: +		}
464: +
465: +		if len(missing) == 0 {
466: +			return
467: +		}
468: +
469: +		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
470: +			t.Fatalf("no %v event for %v", actions, missing)
471: +		}
472: +
473: +		time.Sleep(10 * time.Millisecond)
474: +	}
475: +}
476: +
477: +// newTestNetns creates a named network namespace without leaving the
478: +// current thread in it.
479: +func newTestNetns(t *testing.T) (string, *netlink.Handle) {
480: +	t.Helper()
481: +
482: +	if os.Geteuid() != 0 {
483: +		t.Skip("requires root to create network namespaces")
484: +	}
485: +
486: +	runtime.LockOSThread()
487: +	defer runtime.UnlockOSThread()
488: +
489: +	orig, err := netns.Get()
490: +	if err != nil {
491: +		t.Fatalf("failed to get current netns: %v", err)
492: +	}
493: +	defer orig.Close()
494: +
495: +	name := fmt.Sprintf("clabtst-ev-%d-%d", os.Getpid(), time.Now().UnixNano())
496: +
497: +	ns, err := netns.NewNamed(name)
498: +	if err != nil {
499: +		t.Fatalf("failed to create netns: %v", err)
500: +	}
501: +
502: +	if err := netns.Set(orig); err != nil {
503: +		t.Fatalf("failed to return to the original netns: %v", err)
504: +	}
505: +
506: +	handle, err := netlink.NewHandleAt(ns)
507: +	if err != nil {
508: +		t.Fatalf("failed to create netlink handle: %v", err)
509: +	}
510: +
511: +	t.Cleanup(func() {
512: +		handle.Close()
513: +		ns.Close()
514: +		_ = netns.DeleteNamed(name)
515: +	})
516: +
517: +	return "/run/netns/" + name, handle
518: +}
519: +

524: +	if err != nil {
525: +		t.Errorf("failed to add %s: %v", name, err)
526: +	}
527: +}

550: +func startRegistry(
551: +	t *testing.T,
552: +	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
553: +) (*netlinkRegistry, *eventLog) {
554: +	t.Helper()
555: +
556: +	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
557: +	events := make(chan aggregatedEvent, 1024)
558: +	registry := newNetlinkRegistry(ctx, events, true, false, 0)
559: +	collected := collectEvents(ctx, events)
560: +
561: +	registry.Start(container)
562: +	t.Cleanup(func() {
563: +		registry.Stop(container.ID)
564: +		cancel()
565: +	})
566: +
567: +	return registry, collected
568: +}

570: +// TestNetlinkWatcherReportsExistingAndNewLinks checks that links present at
571: +// subscription are reported as snapshots and later ones as created or deleted.
572: +func TestNetlinkWatcherReportsExistingAndNewLinks(t *testing.T) {
573: +	nsPath, handle := newTestNetns(t)
574: +	addDummy(t, handle, "early0")
575: +

577: +	_, events := startRegistry(t, container)

583: +
584: +	link, err := handle.LinkByName("late0")
585: +	if err != nil {

684: +	link := func(mtu int) netlink.Link {

698: +		},

702: +		"unchanged after resubscribe":         {known: link(1500)},
703: +	}

711: +				includeSnapshot: tt.includeSnapshot,
712: +			}
713: +
714: +			states := map[int]ifaceSnapshot{}
715: +			if tt.known != nil {
716: +				states[7] = snapshotFromLink(tt.known)
717: +			}
718: +
719: +			watcher.processExisting(states, nil, link(1500), nil, registry, tt.initial)
720: +			close(events)
721: +
722: +			var got []string
723: +			for ev := range events {
724: +				got = append(got, ev.Action)
725: +			}
726: +
727: +			if !slices.Equal(got, tt.want) {
728: +				t.Fatalf("actions = %v, want %v", got, tt.want)
729: +			}
730: +
731: +			if states[7].MTU != 1500 {
732: +				t.Fatalf("state MTU = %d, want 1500", states[7].MTU)
733: +			}
734: +		})
735: +	}
736: +}

11: 	"time"
12: 
13: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
14: 	gotc "github.com/florianl/go-tc"
15: 	clabconstants "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/constants"

25: 
26: type netlinkRegistry struct {
27: 	ctx                    context.Context
28: 	mu                     sync.Mutex
29: 	watchers               map[string]*netlinkWatcher
30: 	events                 chan<- aggregatedEvent
31: 	includeInitialSnapshot bool
32: 	includeStats           bool
33: 	statsInterval          time.Duration
34: }
35: 
36: func newNetlinkRegistry(
37: 	ctx context.Context,
38: 	events chan<- aggregatedEvent,
39: 	includeInitialSnapshot bool,
40: 	includeStats bool,
41: 	statsInterval time.Duration,
42: ) *netlinkRegistry {
43: 	return &netlinkRegistry{
44: 		ctx:                    ctx,
45: 		watchers:               make(map[string]*netlinkWatcher),
46: 		events:                 events,
47: 		includeInitialSnapshot: includeInitialSnapshot,
48: 		includeStats:           includeStats,
49: 		statsInterval:          statsInterval,
50: 	}
51: }
52: 
53: func (r *netlinkRegistry) Start(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) {
54: 	clone := cloneContainer(container)
55: 	if clone == nil {
56: 		return
57: 	}
58: 
59: 	id := clone.ID
60: 	if id == "" {
61: 		id = clone.ShortID
62: 	}
63: 
64: 	if id == "" {
65: 		return
66: 	}
67: 
68: 	r.mu.Lock()
69: 	if _, exists := r.watchers[id]; exists {
70: 		r.mu.Unlock()
71: 
72: 		return
73: 	}
74: 
75: 	watcherCtx, cancel := context.WithCancel(r.ctx)
76: 	watcher := &netlinkWatcher{
77: 		container:       clone,
78: 		cancel:          cancel,
79: 		done:            make(chan struct{}),
80: 		includeSnapshot: r.includeInitialSnapshot,
81: 		includeStats:    r.includeStats,
82: 		statsInterval:   r.statsInterval,
83: 	}
84: 
85: 	r.watchers[id] = watcher
86: 	r.mu.Unlock()
87: 
88: 	go watcher.run(watcherCtx, r)
89: }
90: 
91: func (r *netlinkRegistry) Stop(id string) {
92: 	if id == "" {
93: 		return
94: 	}
95: 
96: 	r.mu.Lock()
97: 	watcher, ok := r.watchers[id]
98: 	if ok {
99: 		delete(r.watchers, id)
100: 	}
101: 	r.mu.Unlock()
102: 
103: 	if !ok {
104: 		return
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	watcher.cancel()
108: 	<-watcher.done
109: }
110: 
111: func (r *netlinkRegistry) remove(id string, watcher *netlinkWatcher) {
112: 	if id == "" {
113: 		return
114: 	}
115: 
116: 	r.mu.Lock()
117: 	defer r.mu.Unlock()
118: 
119: 	if current, ok := r.watchers[id]; ok && current == watcher {
120: 		delete(r.watchers, id)
121: 	}
122: }
123: 
124: func (r *netlinkRegistry) HandleContainerEvent(
125: 	runtime clabruntime.ContainerRuntime,
126: 	ev clabruntime.ContainerEvent,
127: ) {
128: 	if !strings.EqualFold(ev.Type, clabruntime.EventTypeContainer) {
129: 		return
130: 	}
131: 
132: 	action := strings.ToLower(ev.Action)
133: 
134: 	switch action {
135: 	case clabruntime.EventActionStart,
136: 		clabruntime.EventActionUnpause,
137: 		clabruntime.EventActionRestart:
138: 		container := containerFromEvent(runtime, ev)
139: 		if container != nil {
140: 			r.Start(container)
141: 		}
142: 	case clabruntime.EventActionDie,
143: 		clabruntime.EventActionStop,
144: 		clabruntime.EventActionDestroy,
145: 		clabruntime.EventActionKill:
146: 		id := ev.ActorFullID
147: 		if id == "" {
148: 			id = ev.ActorID
149: 		}
150: 
151: 		r.Stop(id)
152: 	}
153: }
154: 
155: func cloneContainer(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) *clabruntime.GenericContainer {
156: 	if container == nil {
157: 		return nil
158: 	}
159: 
160: 	clone := &clabruntime.GenericContainer{
161: 		Names:   append([]string{}, container.Names...),
162: 		ID:      container.ID,
163: 		ShortID: container.ShortID,
164: 		Labels:  cloneStringMap(container.Labels),
165: 	}
166: 
167: 	if clone.ShortID == "" {
168: 		clone.ShortID = clabutils.ShortID(clone.ID)
169: 	}
170: 
171: 	if container.Runtime == nil {
172: 		return nil
173: 	}
174: 
175: 	clone.SetRuntime(container.Runtime)
176: 
177: 	return clone
178: }
179: 
180: func containerFromEvent(
181: 	runtime clabruntime.ContainerRuntime,
182: 	ev clabruntime.ContainerEvent,
183: ) *clabruntime.GenericContainer {
184: 	attributes := ev.Attributes
185: 
186: 	name := ev.ActorName
187: 	if name == "" && attributes != nil {
188: 		name = attributes["name"]
189: 	}
190: 
191: 	id := ev.ActorFullID
192: 	if id == "" {
193: 		id = ev.ActorID
194: 	}
195: 
196: 	if id == "" && name == "" {
197: 		return nil
198: 	}
199: 
200: 	short := ev.ActorID
201: 	if short == "" {
202: 		short = id
203: 	}
204: 
205: 	container := &clabruntime.GenericContainer{
206: 		ID:      id,
207: 		ShortID: clabutils.ShortID(short),
208: 	}
209: 
210: 	if name != "" {
211: 		container.Names = []string{name}
212: 	}
213: 
214: 	if attributes != nil {
215: 		for _, key := range []string{
216: 			clabconstants.Containerlab,
217: 			clabconstants.NodeName,
218: 			clabconstants.RootNodeName,
219: 		} {
220: 			if v := attributes[key]; v != "" {
221: 				if container.Labels == nil {
222: 					container.Labels = map[string]string{}
223: 				}
224: 
225: 				container.Labels[key] = v
226: 			}
227: 		}
228: 	}
229: 
230: 	if container.ShortID == "" {
231: 		container.ShortID = clabutils.ShortID(container.ID)
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	if runtime != nil {
235: 		container.SetRuntime(runtime)
236: 	}
237: 
238: 	return container
239: }
240: 
241: type netlinkWatcher struct {
242: 	container       *clabruntime.GenericContainer
243: 	cancel          context.CancelFunc
244: 	done            chan struct{}
245: 	includeSnapshot bool
246: 	includeStats    bool
247: 	statsInterval   time.Duration
248: 	nsHandle        netns.NsHandle
249: }
250: 
251: // Retry settings for a watcher that could not subscribe to link updates.
252: var (
253: 	watchMaxAttempts   = 5
254: 	watchRetryDelay    = time.Second
255: 	watchMaxRetryDelay = 10 * time.Second
256: )
257: 
258: func (w *netlinkWatcher) run(ctx context.Context, registry *netlinkRegistry) {
259: 	defer close(w.done)
260: 	if w.container == nil {
261: 		return
262: 	}
263: 
264: 	defer registry.remove(w.container.ID, w)
265: 
266: 	containerName := firstContainerName(w.container)
267: 	if w.container.Runtime == nil {
268: 		log.Debugf("container %s has no runtime, skipping netlink watcher", containerName)
269: 
270: 		return
271: 	}
272: 
273: 	// The states outlive a subscription, so a resubscribe only reports what
274: 	// changed while no subscription was active.
275: 	states := make(map[int]ifaceSnapshot)
276: 
277: 	var statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample
278: 	if w.includeStats {
279: 		statsSamples = make(map[int]ifaceStatsSample)
280: 	}
281: 
282: 	initial := true
283: 	failures := 0
284: 	delay := watchRetryDelay
285: 
286: 	for {
287: 		subscribed, err := w.watch(ctx, registry, states, statsSamples, initial)
288: 		if ctx.Err() != nil {
289: 			return
290: 		}
291: 
292: 		if subscribed {
293: 			initial = false
294: 			failures = 0
295: 			delay = watchRetryDelay
296: 
297: 			log.Warnf(
298: 				"netlink subscription for container %s ended, resubscribing: %v",
299: 				containerName,
300: 				err,
301: 			)
302: 		} else {
303: 			failures++
304: 			if failures >= watchMaxAttempts {
305: 				log.Warnf("stopped watching interfaces of container %s: %v", containerName, err)
306: 
307: 				return
308: 			}
309: 
310: 			log.Debugf(
311: 				"failed to watch interfaces of container %s, retrying in %s: %v",
312: 				containerName,
313: 				delay,
314: 				err,
315: 			)
316: 		}
317: 
318: 		select {
319: 		case <-time.After(delay):
320: 		case <-ctx.Done():
321: 			return
322: 		}
323: 
324: 		delay = min(delay*2, watchMaxRetryDelay)
325: 	}
326: }
327: 
328: // watch subscribes to link updates in the container's namespace and handles
329: // them until ctx ends or the subscription fails. It reports whether it
330: // subscribed. The subscription lists the existing links itself, so no change
331: // between listing and subscribing is lost.
332: func (w *netlinkWatcher) watch(
333: 	ctx context.Context,
334: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
335: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
336: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
337: 	initial bool,
338: ) (bool, error) {
339: 	nsPath, err := waitForNamespacePath(ctx, w.container.Runtime, w.container.ID)
340: 	if err != nil {
341: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to resolve netns: %w", err)
342: 	}
343: 
344: 	nsHandle, err := netns.GetFromPath(nsPath)
345: 	if err != nil {
346: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to open netns: %w", err)
347: 	}
348: 	defer nsHandle.Close()
349: 
350: 	w.nsHandle = nsHandle
351: 
352: 	netHandle, err := netlink.NewHandleAt(nsHandle)
353: 	if err != nil {
354: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to create netlink handle: %w", err)
355: 	}
356: 	defer netHandle.Close()
357: 
358: 	netemQuery := newNetemQuerier(nsHandle)
359: 	defer netemQuery.Close()
360: 
361: 	netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()
362: 
363: 	updates := make(chan netlink.LinkUpdate, 32)
364: 	done := make(chan struct{})
365: 	defer close(done)
366: 
367: 	var (
368: 		errMu     sync.Mutex
369: 		lastError error
370: 	)
371: 
372: 	opts := netlink.LinkSubscribeOptions{
373: 		Namespace:    &nsHandle,
374: 		ListExisting: true,
375: 		// An interrupted listing is reported here too. It needs no action:
376: 		// links that changed during the listing also arrive as updates.
377: 		ErrorCallback: func(err error) {
378: 			log.Debugf(
379: 				"netlink subscription for container %s: %v",
380: 				firstContainerName(w.container),
381: 				err,
382: 			)
383: 
384: 			errMu.Lock()
385: 			lastError = err
386: 			errMu.Unlock()
387: 		},
388: 	}
389: 
390: 	if err := netlink.LinkSubscribeWithOptions(updates, done, opts); err != nil {
391: 		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to subscribe to netlink updates: %w", err)
392: 	}
393: 
394: 	if len(states) > 0 {
395: 		w.removeVanishedLinks(netHandle, states, statsSamples, registry)
396: 	}
397: 
398: 	// Set up netem polling interval (reuse stats interval or default to 1s).
399: 	netemPollInterval := w.statsInterval
400: 	if netemPollInterval == 0 {
401: 		netemPollInterval = time.Second
402: 	}
403: 	netemTicker := time.NewTicker(netemPollInterval)
404: 	defer netemTicker.Stop()
405: 
406: 	var (
407: 		ticker  *time.Ticker
408: 		tickerC <-chan time.Time
409: 	)
410: 	if w.includeStats && w.statsInterval > 0 {
411: 		ticker = time.NewTicker(w.statsInterval)
412: 		tickerC = ticker.C
413: 		defer ticker.Stop()
414: 	}
415: 
416: 	for {
417: 		select {
418: 		case <-tickerC:
419: 			w.collectAndEmitStats(netHandle, states, statsSamples, registry)
420: 		case <-netemTicker.C:
421: 			w.pollNetemChanges(states, registry, netemQuery)
422: 		case <-ctx.Done():
423: 			return true, nil
424: 		case update, ok := <-updates:
425: 			if !ok {
426: 				errMu.Lock()
427: 				err := lastError
428: 				errMu.Unlock()
429: 
430: 				if err == nil {
431: 					err = errors.New("update channel closed")
432: 				}
433: 
434: 				return true, err
435: 			}
436: 
437: 			// Replies to the listing carry NLM_F_MULTI, notifications do not.
438: 			if update.Header.Flags&unix.NLM_F_MULTI != 0 {
439: 				w.processExisting(states, statsSamples, update.Link, netemInfos, registry, initial)
440: 
441: 				continue
442: 			}
443: 
444: 			w.processUpdate(states, statsSamples, update, registry, netemQuery)
445: 		}
446: 	}
447: }
448: 
449: // processExisting records a link listed when the subscription started. On the
450: // first subscription a new link is reported as a snapshot when requested; after
451: // a resubscribe it is reported as created.
452: func (w *netlinkWatcher) processExisting(
453: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
454: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
455: 	link netlink.Link,
456: 	netemInfos map[int]netemInfo,
457: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
458: 	initial bool,
459: ) {
460: 	if link == nil || link.Attrs() == nil {
461: 		return
462: 	}
463: 
464: 	snapshot := withNetem(snapshotFromLink(link), netemInfos)
465: 	previous, exists := states[snapshot.Index]
466: 
467: 	if exists && snapshot.equal(previous) {
468: 		return
469: 	}
470: 
471: 	states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
472: 	if statsSamples != nil {
473: 		if sample, ok := newStatsSample(snapshot, time.Now()); ok {
474: 			statsSamples[snapshot.Index] = sample
475: 		}
476: 	}
477: 
478: 	switch {
479: 	case exists:
480: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "update", snapshot)
481: 	case !initial:
482: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "create", snapshot)
483: 	case w.includeSnapshot:
484: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "snapshot", snapshot)
485: 	}
486: }
487: 
488: // removeVanishedLinks reports the links that were deleted while no
489: // subscription was active.
490: func (w *netlinkWatcher) removeVanishedLinks(
491: 	netHandle *netlink.Handle,
492: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
493: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
494: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
495: ) {
496: 	current, err := snapshotInterfaces(netHandle)
497: 	if err != nil {
498: 		log.Debugf(
499: 			"failed to list interfaces of container %s: %v",
500: 			firstContainerName(w.container),
501: 			err,
502: 		)
503: 
504: 		return
505: 	}
506: 
507: 	for idx, snapshot := range states {
508: 		if _, ok := current[idx]; ok {
509: 			continue
510: 		}
511: 
512: 		delete(states, idx)
513: 		delete(statsSamples, idx)
514: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "delete", snapshot)
515: 	}
516: }
517: 
518: func (w *netlinkWatcher) processUpdate(
519: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
520: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
521: 	update netlink.LinkUpdate,
522: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
523: 	netemQuery *netemQuerier,
524: ) {
525: 	if update.Link == nil {
526: 		return
527: 	}
528: 
529: 	attrs := update.Link.Attrs()
530: 	if attrs == nil {
531: 		return
532: 	}
533: 
534: 	snapshot := snapshotFromLink(update.Link)
535: 	previous, exists := states[snapshot.Index]
536: 
537: 	switch update.Header.Type {
538: 	case unix.RTM_DELLINK:
539: 		// A link that is not tracked was never reported, or its deletion was
540: 		// already reported when the links were listed again after a resubscribe.
541: 		if !exists {
542: 			return
543: 		}
544: 
545: 		delete(states, snapshot.Index)
546: 		delete(statsSamples, snapshot.Index)
547: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "delete", previous)
548: 	case unix.RTM_NEWLINK:
549: 		// Query netem info for this interface
550: 		snapshot = withNetem(snapshot, netemQuery.Query())
551: 
552: 		if exists && snapshot.equal(previous) {
553: 			return
554: 		}
555: 
556: 		action := "create"
557: 		if exists {
558: 			action = "update"
559: 		}
560: 
561: 		states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
562: 		if w.includeStats && statsSamples != nil {
563: 			if sample, ok := newStatsSample(snapshot, time.Now()); ok {
564: 				statsSamples[snapshot.Index] = sample
565: 			}
566: 		}
567: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, action, snapshot)
568: 	}
569: }
570: 
571: // pollNetemChanges checks for netem changes and emits update events.
572: func (w *netlinkWatcher) pollNetemChanges(
573: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
574: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
575: 	netemQuery *netemQuerier,
576: ) {
577: 	netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()
578: 
579: 	for idx, snapshot := range states {
580: 		previous := snapshot
581: 		info, hasNetem := netemInfos[idx]
582: 
583: 		if hasNetem {
584: 			snapshot.HasNetem = true
585: 			snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
586: 			snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
587: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
588: 			snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
589: 			snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
590: 		} else if snapshot.HasNetem {
591: 			// Netem was removed
592: 			snapshot.HasNetem = false
593: 			snapshot.Delay = ""
594: 			snapshot.Jitter = ""
595: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = 0
596: 			snapshot.Rate = 0
597: 			snapshot.Corruption = 0
598: 		}
599: 
600: 		if !snapshot.equal(previous) {
601: 			states[idx] = snapshot
602: 			registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "update", snapshot)
603: 		}
604: 	}
605: }
606: 
607: func firstContainerName(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) string {
608: 	if container == nil || len(container.Names) == 0 {
609: 		return ""
610: 	}
611: 
612: 	return container.Names[0]
613: }
614: 
615: func containerLabel(container *clabruntime.GenericContainer) string {
616: 	if container == nil || container.Labels == nil {
617: 		return ""
618: 	}
619: 
620: 	return container.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
621: }
622: 
623: func interfaceAttributes(
624: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
625: 	snapshot ifaceSnapshot,
626: ) map[string]string {
627: 	attributes := map[string]string{
628: 		"ifname": snapshot.Name,
629: 		"index":  strconv.Itoa(snapshot.Index),
630: 		"mtu":    strconv.Itoa(snapshot.MTU),
631: 		"state":  snapshot.OperState,
632: 		"type":   snapshot.Type,
633: 		"origin": "netlink",
634: 	}
635: 
636: 	if snapshot.Alias != "" {
637: 		attributes["alias"] = snapshot.Alias
638: 	}
639: 
640: 	if snapshot.MAC != "" {
641: 		attributes["mac"] = snapshot.MAC
642: 	}
643: 
644: 	if lab := containerLabel(container); lab != "" {
645: 		attributes["lab"] = lab
646: 	}
647: 
648: 	if name := firstContainerName(container); name != "" {
649: 		attributes["name"] = name
650: 	}
651: 
652: 	overlayToolsInterface(container, attributes)
653: 
654: 	// Add netem attributes if present
655: 	if snapshot.HasNetem {
656: 		if snapshot.Delay != "" {
657: 			attributes["netem_delay"] = snapshot.Delay
658: 		}
659: 		if snapshot.Jitter != "" {
660: 			attributes["netem_jitter"] = snapshot.Jitter
661: 		}
662: 		if snapshot.PacketLoss != 0 {
663: 			attributes["netem_loss"] = strconv.FormatFloat(snapshot.PacketLoss, 'f', 2, 64) + "%"
664: 		}
665: 		if snapshot.Rate != 0 {
666: 			attributes["netem_rate"] = strconv.Itoa(snapshot.Rate) + "kbit"
667: 		}
668: 		if snapshot.Corruption != 0 {
669: 			attributes["netem_corruption"] = strconv.FormatFloat(
670: 				snapshot.Corruption,
671: 				'f',
672: 				2,
673: 				64,
674: 			) + "%"
675: 		}
676: 	}
677: 
678: 	return attributes
679: }
680: 
681: // overlayToolsInterface rewrites attributes to the host-side stitch interface
682: // when one exists, matching `inspect interfaces`.
683: func overlayToolsInterface(
684: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
685: 	attributes map[string]string,
686: ) {
687: 	if container == nil {
688: 		return
689: 	}
690: 
691: 	lab, node := clabcore.TopoIdentity(container.Labels)
692: 	if lab == "" || node == "" {
693: 		return
694: 	}
695: 
696: 	topoName := attributes["alias"]
697: 	if topoName == "" {
698: 		topoName = attributes["ifname"]
699: 	}

746: func waitForNamespacePath(
747: 	ctx context.Context,
748: 	runtime clabruntime.ContainerRuntime,
749: 	containerID string,
750: ) (string, error) {
751: 	const (
752: 		attempts   = 5
753: 		retryDelay = 200 * time.Millisecond
754: 	)
755: 
756: 	var lastErr error
757: 
758: 	for i := 0; i < attempts; i++ {
759: 		nsPath, err := runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, containerID)
760: 		if err == nil && nsPath != "" {
761: 			return nsPath, nil
762: 		}
763: 
764: 		if err != nil {
765: 			lastErr = err
766: 		}
767: 
768: 		select {
769: 		case <-time.After(retryDelay):
770: 		case <-ctx.Done():
771: 			return "", ctx.Err()
772: 		}
773: 	}
774: 
775: 	if lastErr != nil {
776: 		return "", lastErr
777: 	}
778: 
779: 	return "", fmt.Errorf("namespace path not ready for container %s", containerID)
780: }

818: func snapshotInterfaces(netHandle *netlink.Handle) (map[int]ifaceSnapshot, error) {
819: 	if netHandle == nil {
820: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netlink handle is nil")
821: 	}
822: 
823: 	links, err := netHandle.LinkList()
824: 	if err != nil {
825: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to list links: %w", err)
826: 	}
827: 
828: 	states := make(map[int]ifaceSnapshot, len(links))
829: 	for _, link := range links {
830: 		snapshot := snapshotFromLink(link)
831: 		states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
832: 	}
833: 
834: 	return states, nil
835: }

837: // withNetem returns the snapshot with the netem settings of its interface.
838: func withNetem(snapshot ifaceSnapshot, netemInfos map[int]netemInfo) ifaceSnapshot {
839: 	if info, ok := netemInfos[snapshot.Index]; ok {
840: 		snapshot.HasNetem = true
841: 		snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
842: 		snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
843: 		snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
844: 		snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
845: 		snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
846: 	}
847: 
848: 	return snapshot
849: }

851: func snapshotFromLink(link netlink.Link) ifaceSnapshot {
852: 	attrs := link.Attrs()
853: 
854: 	snapshot := ifaceSnapshot{
855: 		Type: link.Type(),
856: 	}
857: 
858: 	if attrs != nil {
859: 		snapshot.Index = attrs.Index
860: 		snapshot.Name = attrs.Name
861: 		snapshot.Alias = attrs.Alias
862: 		snapshot.MTU = attrs.MTU
863: 		if len(attrs.HardwareAddr) > 0 {
864: 			snapshot.MAC = attrs.HardwareAddr.String()
865: 		}
866: 		snapshot.OperState = attrs.OperState.String()
867: 		if stats := attrs.Statistics; stats != nil {
868: 			snapshot.HasStats = true
869: 			snapshot.RxBytes = stats.RxBytes
870: 			snapshot.TxBytes = stats.TxBytes
871: 			snapshot.RxPackets = stats.RxPackets
872: 			snapshot.TxPackets = stats.TxPackets
873: 		}
874: 	}
875: 
876: 	return snapshot
877: }

938: func newStatsSample(snapshot ifaceSnapshot, timestamp time.Time) (ifaceStatsSample, bool) {
939: 	if !snapshot.HasStats {
940: 		return ifaceStatsSample{}, false
941: 	}
942: 
943: 	return ifaceStatsSample{
944: 		RxBytes:   snapshot.RxBytes,
945: 		TxBytes:   snapshot.TxBytes,
946: 		RxPackets: snapshot.RxPackets,
947: 		TxPackets: snapshot.TxPackets,
948: 		Timestamp: timestamp,
949: 	}, true
950: }

952: func (w *netlinkWatcher) collectAndEmitStats(
953: 	netHandle *netlink.Handle,
954: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
955: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
956: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
957: ) {
958: 	if netHandle == nil || statsSamples == nil {
959: 		return
960: 	}
961: 
962: 	now := time.Now()
963: 	// Fetch all interfaces with one netlink call per tick instead of one call
964: 	// per interface to reduce syscall overhead in large labs.
965: 	currentStates, err := snapshotInterfaces(netHandle)
966: 	if err != nil {
967: 		log.Debugf(
968: 			"failed to collect interface stats snapshot for container %s: %v",
969: 			firstContainerName(w.container),
970: 			err,
971: 		)
972: 
973: 		return
974: 	}
975: 
976: 	for idx, state := range states {
977: 		current, ok := currentStates[idx]
978: 		if !ok {
979: 			continue
980: 		}
981: 
982: 		state.Name = current.Name
983: 		state.Alias = current.Alias
984: 		state.MTU = current.MTU
985: 		state.MAC = current.MAC
986: 		state.OperState = current.OperState
987: 		state.Type = current.Type
988: 		state.HasStats = current.HasStats
989: 		state.RxBytes = current.RxBytes
990: 		state.TxBytes = current.TxBytes
991: 		state.RxPackets = current.RxPackets
992: 		state.TxPackets = current.TxPackets
993: 		states[idx] = state
994: 
995: 		if !state.HasStats {
996: 			delete(statsSamples, idx)
997: 
998: 			continue
999: 		}
1000: 
1001: 		prev, ok := statsSamples[idx]
1002: 		if !ok || now.Sub(prev.Timestamp) <= 0 {
1003: 			statsSamples[idx] = ifaceStatsSample{
1004: 				RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1005: 				TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1006: 				RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1007: 				TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1008: 				Timestamp: now,
1009: 			}
1010: 
1011: 			continue
1012: 		}
1013: 
1014: 		interval := now.Sub(prev.Timestamp)
1015: 		if interval <= 0 {
1016: 			statsSamples[idx] = ifaceStatsSample{
1017: 				RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1018: 				TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1019: 				RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1020: 				TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1021: 				Timestamp: now,
1022: 			}
1023: 
1024: 			continue
1025: 		}
1026: 
1027: 		rxBytesDelta := deltaCounter(prev.RxBytes, state.RxBytes)
1028: 		txBytesDelta := deltaCounter(prev.TxBytes, state.TxBytes)
1029: 		rxPacketsDelta := deltaCounter(prev.RxPackets, state.RxPackets)
1030: 		txPacketsDelta := deltaCounter(prev.TxPackets, state.TxPackets)
1031: 
1032: 		seconds := interval.Seconds()
1033: 		if seconds <= 0 {
1034: 			statsSamples[idx] = ifaceStatsSample{
1035: 				RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1036: 				TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1037: 				RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1038: 				TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1039: 				Timestamp: now,
1040: 			}
1041: 
1042: 			continue
1043: 		}
1044: 
1045: 		metrics := ifaceStatsMetrics{
1046: 			RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1047: 			TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1048: 			RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1049: 			TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1050: 			RxBps:     float64(rxBytesDelta) * 8 / seconds,
1051: 			TxBps:     float64(txBytesDelta) * 8 / seconds,
1052: 			RxPps:     float64(rxPacketsDelta) / seconds,
1053: 			TxPps:     float64(txPacketsDelta) / seconds,
1054: 			Interval:  interval,
1055: 			Timestamp: now,
1056: 		}
1057: 
1058: 		registry.emitInterfaceStatsEvent(w.container, state, metrics)
1059: 
1060: 		statsSamples[idx] = ifaceStatsSample{
1061: 			RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1062: 			TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1063: 			RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1064: 			TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1065: 			Timestamp: now,
1066: 		}
1067: 	}
1068: }
1069: 
1070: func deltaCounter(previous, current uint64) uint64 {
1071: 	if current >= previous {
1072: 		return current - previous
1073: 	}
1074: 
1075: 	return current
1076: }

1080: )

1082: type netemQuerier struct {
1083: 	nsHandle netns.NsHandle
1084: 	tcHandle *gotc.Tc
1085: }
1086: 
1087: func newNetemQuerier(nsHandle netns.NsHandle) *netemQuerier {
1088: 	return &netemQuerier{
1089: 		nsHandle: nsHandle,
1090: 	}

1092: 
1093: func (q *netemQuerier) Close() {
1094: 	if q == nil || q.tcHandle == nil {
1095: 		return
1096: 	}
1097: 
1098: 	q.tcHandle.Close()
1099: 	q.tcHandle = nil
1100: }
1101: 
1102: // Query queries the TC qdiscs in the namespace and returns a map of
1103: // interface index to netem data.
1104: func (q *netemQuerier) Query() map[int]netemInfo {
1105: 	result := make(map[int]netemInfo)
1106: 
1107: 	qdiscs, err := q.qdiscs()
1108: 	if err != nil {
1109: 		log.Debugf("failed to query tc qdiscs: %v", err)
1110: 		return result
1111: 	}
1112: 
1113: 	for idx := range qdiscs {
1114: 		qdisc := &qdiscs[idx]
1115: 		if qdisc.Attribute.Kind != "netem" || qdisc.Netem == nil {
1116: 			continue
1117: 		}
1118: 
1119: 		info := netemInfoFromQdisc(qdisc)
1120: 		if info.hasValues() {
1121: 			result[int(qdisc.Ifindex)] = info
1122: 		}
1123: 	}
1124: 
1125: 	return result
1126: }
1127: 
1128: func (q *netemQuerier) qdiscs() ([]gotc.Object, error) {
1129: 	if q == nil {
1130: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem querier is nil")
1131: 	}
1132: 
1133: 	if err := q.ensureHandle(); err != nil {
1134: 		return nil, err
1135: 	}
1136: 
1137: 	qdiscs, err := clabnetem.Impairments(q.tcHandle)
1138: 	if err == nil {
1139: 		return qdiscs, nil
1140: 	}
1141: 
1142: 	// Retry once by reopening the tc socket in case it became stale.
1143: 	q.Close()
1144: 	if errOpen := q.ensureHandle(); errOpen != nil {
1145: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
1146: 			"failed to query tc qdiscs (%v), and failed to reopen tc socket (%v)",
1147: 			err,
1148: 			errOpen,
1149: 		)
1150: 	}
1151: 
1152: 	return clabnetem.Impairments(q.tcHandle)
1153: }
1154: 
1155: func (q *netemQuerier) ensureHandle() error {
1156: 	if q == nil {
1157: 		return fmt.Errorf("netem querier is nil")
1158: 	}
1159: 
1160: 	if q.tcHandle != nil {
1161: 		return nil
1162: 	}
1163: 
1164: 	tcnl, err := clabnetem.NewTC(int(q.nsHandle))
1165: 	if err != nil {
1166: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to open tc socket for netem query: %w", err)
1167: 	}
1168: 
1169: 	q.tcHandle = tcnl
1170: 
1171: 	return nil
1172: }
1173: 
1174: type netemInfo struct {
1175: 	Delay      string
1176: 	Jitter     string
1177: 	PacketLoss float64
1178: 	Rate       int
1179: 	Corruption float64
1180: }
1181: 
1182: func (n netemInfo) hasValues() bool {
1183: 	return n.Delay != "" || n.Jitter != "" || n.PacketLoss != 0 || n.Rate != 0 || n.Corruption != 0
1184: }
1185: 
1186: func netemInfoFromQdisc(qdisc *gotc.Object) netemInfo {
1187: 	var info netemInfo
1188: 
1189: 	if qdisc.Netem == nil {
1190: 		return info
1191: 	}
1192: 
1193: 	if qdisc.Netem.Latency64 != nil && *qdisc.Netem.Latency64 != 0 {
1194: 		info.Delay = (time.Duration(*qdisc.Netem.Latency64) * time.Nanosecond).String()
1195: 	}
1196: 
1197: 	if qdisc.Netem.Jitter64 != nil && *qdisc.Netem.Jitter64 != 0 {
1198: 		info.Jitter = (time.Duration(*qdisc.Netem.Jitter64) * time.Nanosecond).String()
1199: 	}
1200: 
1201: 	if qdisc.Netem.Rate != nil && qdisc.Netem.Rate.Rate != 0 {
1202: 		info.Rate = int(qdisc.Netem.Rate.Rate * 8 / msPerSec)
1203: 	}
1204: 
1205: 	if qdisc.Netem.Corrupt != nil && qdisc.Netem.Corrupt.Probability != 0 {
1206: 		info.Corruption = math.Round((float64(qdisc.Netem.Corrupt.Probability)/
1207: 			float64(math.MaxUint32)*100)*100) / 100
1208: 	}
1209: 
1210: 	if qdisc.Netem.Qopt.Loss != 0 {
1211: 		info.PacketLoss = math.Round(
1212: 			(float64(qdisc.Netem.Qopt.Loss)/float64(math.MaxUint32)*100)*100) / 100
1213: 	}
1214: 
1215: 	return info
1216: }

541: // overlayToolsInterface rewrites attributes to the host-side stitch interface
542: // when one exists, matching `inspect interfaces`.
543: func overlayToolsInterface(
544: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
545: 	attributes map[string]string,
546: ) {
547: 	if container == nil {

606: func waitForNamespacePath(
607: 	ctx context.Context,
608: 	runtime clabruntime.ContainerRuntime,
609: 	containerID string,
610: ) (string, error) {
611: 	const (
612: 		attempts   = 5
613: 		retryDelay = 200 * time.Millisecond
614: 	)
615: 
616: 	var lastErr error
617: 
618: 	for i := 0; i < attempts; i++ {
619: 		nsPath, err := runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, containerID)
620: 		if err == nil && nsPath != "" {
621: 			return nsPath, nil
622: 		}
623: 
624: 		if err != nil {
625: 			lastErr = err
626: 		}
627: 
628: 		select {
629: 		case <-time.After(retryDelay):
630: 		case <-ctx.Done():
631: 			return "", ctx.Err()
632: 		}
633: 	}
634: 
635: 	if lastErr != nil {
636: 		return "", lastErr
637: 	}
638: 
639: 	return "", fmt.Errorf("namespace path not ready for container %s", containerID)
640: }

678: func snapshotInterfaces(netHandle *netlink.Handle) (map[int]ifaceSnapshot, error) {
679: 	if netHandle == nil {
680: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netlink handle is nil")
681: 	}
682: 
683: 	links, err := netHandle.LinkList()
684: 	if err != nil {
685: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to list links: %w", err)
686: 	}
687: 
688: 	states := make(map[int]ifaceSnapshot, len(links))
689: 	for _, link := range links {
690: 		snapshot := snapshotFromLink(link)
691: 		states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
692: 	}
693: 
694: 	return states, nil
695: }
696: 
697: // applyNetemToSnapshots applies netem information to interface snapshots.
698: func applyNetemToSnapshots(states map[int]ifaceSnapshot, netemInfos map[int]netemInfo) {
699: 	for idx, info := range netemInfos {
700: 		if snapshot, ok := states[idx]; ok {
701: 			snapshot.HasNetem = true
702: 			snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
703: 			snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
704: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
705: 			snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
706: 			snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
707: 			states[idx] = snapshot
708: 		}
709: 	}
710: }

Reasoning
Now I have a good picture of the changes. Let me analyze the logic for issues.

Key behavior changes:
1. `run` now retries subscription failures with backoff (up to 5 attempts, delay from 1s doubling to max 10s), and resubscribes when the subscription ends (channel closed or ctx done... wait, on ctx done `watch` returns `true, nil` and run checks `ctx.Err() != nil` → returns).
2. States map now persists across subscriptions; `processExisting` handles listed links; `removeVanishedLinks` handles deletes during unsubscribed gaps.
3. `processExisting` uses `NLM_F_MULTI` flag to distinguish listing replies from notifications.
4. `processUpdate` RTM_DELLINK: now ignores untracked links (before it emitted a delete for untracked links — well, with a zero snapshot if !exists... actually before, if !exists, snapshot was from update.Link, and it emitted a delete event; now it skips).
5. New test file.

Potential issues:

**A. `NLM_F_MULTI` heuristic for distinguishing listing replies from notifications.**

`netlink.LinkSubscribeOptions{ListExisting: true}` — how does vishvananda/netlink implement this? Let me check the vendored netlink code. In `netlink.LinkSubscribeWithOptions`, when ListExisting is true, it does a dump request (RTM_GETLINK with NLM_F_DUMP which sets NLM_F_MULTI | NLM_F_REQUEST | NLM_F_DUMP). Replies to a dump carry NLM_F_MULTI... Actually, kernel sets NLM_F_MULTI on each message of a multi-part reply, and the final message has NLMSG_DONE. Notifications (async RTM_NEWLINK via RTMGRP_LINK multicast groups) do NOT set NLM_F_MULTI. So the heuristic is: listing replies have NLM_F_MULTI set.

But wait — is this robust? The subscribe socket is joined to RTNLGRP_LINK multicast group. The dump replies come on the same socket. Kernel replies to a dump request: each netlink message in the dump has NLM_F_MULTI flag set. Multicast notifications for link events do not set NLM_F_MULTI. That's generally correct.

However, there's a subtlety: some link notifications can be NLM_F_MULTI? No — kernel-generated notifications don't set MULTI. But there could also be the issue that the dump replies arrive intermixed with notifications; that's fine.

But actually there's a deeper concern: the netlink library's `LinkSubscribeWithOptions` with ListExisting does its own dump internally and delivers parsed LinkUpdate on the channel — all messages including the dump replies. Let me check the actual library code. In vishvananda/netlink, `LinkSubscribeWithOptions`: if `opts.ListExisting` is set, it sends RTM_GETLINK dump to the socket... Actually let me check: in the library, `linkSubscribeOptions` — the subscribe function creates a socket, joins groups, then if ListExisting is true, it calls `LinkList` on that socket? Let me check the vendored code.

Let me look for vendor directory in the snapshot.

Actually the manifest inventory — I should check whether vendored netlink is included. Let me glob.

**B. `processExisting` netem query staleness.** `netemInfos` is queried once before subscription; `processExisting` applies those netemInfos to listed links. That's fine.

But wait — `withNetem` sets `HasNetem` and values only if the interface has netem. If netem was removed between subscriptions... pollNetemChanges handles that. OK.

**C. Race: `removeVanishedLinks` is called after `LinkSubscribeWithOptions`, listing current links. Between subscription start and the listing, a delete notification may arrive and be processed... Actually the updates channel has buffer 32; the subscribe goroutine writes to it. `processExisting` handles listed links (NLM_F_MULTI), `processUpdate` handles notifications. If a link was deleted between the end of the previous subscription and the new listing: `removeVanishedLinks` reports delete. If the delete notification also arrives on the channel, `processUpdate` sees !exists → return. Good.

But there's a race the other way: a link is created during the gap (before subscription). It's not in states. Listing (ListExisting) will include it as NLM_F_MULTI → processExisting: exists=false, initial=false → "create". Good. And a notification for that link's creation (RTM_NEWLINK sent while not subscribed is lost, so no dup). But if the link was created *just as* the subscription started, both the listing reply and a notification could arrive → first processed as processExisting (create), then the notification: processUpdate RTM_NEWLINK → exists && equal → return. Good, dedup handled.

During that gap, changes are missed but recovered on resubscribe via ListExisting + removeVanishedLinks... but only state changes are recovered; the events during the gap are simply not emitted (they're synthesized on resubscribe as create/delete/update). If the first subscription ended without emitting snapshots (e.g., failed immediately), the client may never get the initial snapshot. That's a degradation in an edge case. How likely? The subscription could fail quickly if the netns disappears right after opening the handle. Then listing interrupted (ErrorCallback) → channel closes → resubscribe → links reported as create. Since `includeSnapshot` semantics ("includeInitialSnapshot") is about the events command showing current state at start, reporting them as "create" is arguably fine-ish but changes documented semantics. Is there a doc for events command? docs/cmd/events.md exists (in manifest, unchanged). Let me check what it says about snapshot action. Minor P4/P3 at most.

Actually more important: on the FIRST subscription, if `initial=true` and `includeSnapshot=false`, listed links emit nothing. After resubscribe with initial=false, ALL listed links not in states → "create". But states persist, so links known → nothing. Good.

**PP. Now let's verify the `NLM_F_MULTI` claim by checking the vendored library, and also check whether `LinkSubscribeWithOptions` with ListExisting delivers the dump replies *through the same channel with MULTI flags preserved*. One caveat: the library's link parsing might set `update.Header` from the received message header — yes, `update := LinkUpdate{Header: msg.Header, ...}`.

Another caveat: does the library filter NLMSG_DONE? The DONE message has Type NLMSG_DONE; library parses `LinkUpdate` only for RTM_NEWLINK? Let me check: in subscribe handling, the library does:

```go
for _, m := range msgs {
    if m.Header.Type == unix.NLMSG_DONE || m.Header.Type == unix.NLMSG_ERROR {
        continue?  
    }
}
```

Hmm. Actually for LinkSubscribe the library code is:

```go
		for _, m := range msgs {
			if m.Header.Type == unix.NLMSG_DONE {
				// End of a multi-part message; nothing to do.
			}
```

I really need the source. Let me search the snapshot for the vendored netlink.
